## Lead C: AGI contractual determination and contemporaneous capability knowledge

**2023-25 (DOCUMENTED CONTRACTUAL INTEREST)**: CMA described IP licensing carve-outs relating to AGI and revenue-share arrangements. Microsoft's Oct. 28, 2025 SEC-filed partnership disclosure described a newly added independent-expert verification process for an OpenAI AGI declaration, changing rights to research IP and revenue sharing; some IP licenses extended through 2032. This creates financially meaningful questions about AGI declarations. Do not assume the 2025 revised terms were identical to the 2023 contract.

**Nov. 22-23, 2023 (CONTEMPORANEOUS REPORTED ALLEGATION)**: Reuters cited anonymous people describing a letter to the board warning about model capability (Q*). Reuters did not have the letter or independently verify the capabilities. Treat as a substantial source lead, not as a transcript of a verified warning or proof AGI existed.

**OPEN central test**: Acquire dated 2023 contract and side letters defining AGI; authority to certify; model evaluations/alerts; board distributions and votes; licensing/revenue-rights communications; relevant 2023 communications to Microsoft. Determine whether any required representation was false when made and whether that falsity was material to an identified transfer or payment.

**Sources:**
- CMA decision above.
- SEC-filed Microsoft exhibit, Oct. 28, 2025: https://www.sec.gov/Archives/edgar/data/789019/000119312525256310/msft-ex99_2.htm
- Reuters Nov. 22, 2023: https://uk.investing.com/news/stock-market-news/exclusivesam-altmans-ouster-at-openai-was-precipitated-by-letter-to-board-about-ai-breakthrough-sources-3246958

## Lead D: Restrictive contracts, securities whistleblowers, and possible retaliation predicates

**July 1, 2024 (DOCUMENTED COMPLAINT; ALLEGED VIOLATION)**: Attorneys Kohn, Kohn & Colapinto sent SEC Chairman Gary Gensler a seven-page letter on behalf of anonymous whistleblowers who said they filed an SEC tip/complaint. It alleged nondisparagement and confidentiality provisions with no SEC-reporting exceptions, prior-consent requirements, notification requirements, and waivers of whistleblower awards. These are allegations concerning Rule 21F-17(a), not a published SEC finding against OpenAI.

**Potential criminal screening**: The lawyers expressly asked the SEC to determine whether any employee was harmed for truthful reports to federal law enforcement in violation of 18 U.S.C. 1513(e). Section 1513 is expressly included among the enumerated predicates of RICO under 18 U.S.C. 1961(1). An unlawful SEC-gag agreement alone is not proof of section 1513(e); actual retaliatory action with statutory intent and nexus must be shown. Section 1512 may be a separate inquiry only on evidence of corrupt intent to impede qualifying communication.

**Contemporaneous policy changes and contrary evidence**: OpenAI's May and July 2024 communications and its July 31 written response said it had voided nondisparagement restrictions, updated templates, and never canceled vested equity; former employees described the original agreements as coercive before those changes. Verify both historical contract language and the actual treatment of identified individuals before and after protected reports.

**OPEN central test**: Obtain all template versions and executed agreements 2021-24, equity repurchase decisions, actual threatened or imposed loss, employee regulatory reports with protected confidentiality, management knowledge, and time-indexed adverse actions. The SEC's procedural response and any enforcement disposition remain unestablished by this research.

**Sources:**
- Full law firm letter: https://kkc.com/wp-content/uploads/2024/07/Confidential-Letter-to-SEC-Chair-7.1.24_Redacted-1.pdf
- Senate Grassley correspondence Aug. 1, 2024: https://www.grassley.senate.gov/download/grassley-to-openai_-ndas?download=1
- July 31 OpenAI signed reply above.
- Statute 18 USC 1961: https://uscode.house.gov/view.xhtml?req=granuleid:USC-prelim-title18-section1961&num=0&edition=prelim

## Lead E: Insider financial interests, related-party decisions, and oversight

**May 8, 2026 (DOCUMENTED CONGRESSIONAL INQUIRY)**: House Oversight Chairman Comer sent an information demand to Altman asking about an allegedly proposed $500 million OpenAI equity investment in Helion at approximately $35 billion valuation, given Altman's personal Helion stake. The committee also raised interests involving Greg Brockman and Altman's family fund. House request sought conflict procedures, audit materials, and communications from 2015 onward.

**May 2026 (COURT DISCLOSURE, REPORTED)**: Reuters reported financial disclosures of over $2 billion in Altman-held stakes across nine companies with OpenAI business relationships; Altman described recusals. An OpenAI investment in Helion equity at the proposed terms was reportedly rejected; a separate energy purchase/offtake deal was entered. Distinguish proposal from completed transaction.

**Additional financial evidence:** Reuters reported Altman testified he initiated exploration of OpenAI-Helion business in late 2022 and advocated Helion as a good deal; he also participated in discussing a 2024 Reddit partnership while holding a stake, with the board approving final terms. This means the definition and timing of recusal (originating versus negotiation versus final approval) require scrutiny; an asserted final-decision recusal does not prove no earlier influence. Reuters reported Brockman holds interests in both Helion and Cerebras and a percentage of Altman family fund.

**OPEN central test**: Corporate conflict register, board/committee attendance and abstention logs, alternative bids, fairness review, energy purchase contract pricing, stake valuations around negotiation/announcement, and direct investor/public representations. If robust independent decision-making, fair valuations, and disclosure are established, self-dealing allegations are materially weakened. The existence of an investment by itself establishes financial interest, not theft, bribery, or a wire-fraud scheme.

**Sources:**
- House Oversight inquiry original PDF: https://oversight.house.gov/wp-content/uploads/2026/05/Altman-OpenAI-Letter-050826.pdf
- Reuters May 13, 2026 financial disclosure and Altman testimony: https://www.investing.com/news/economy-news/openai-chief-altman-has-over-2-billion-stake-in-companies-that-dealt-with-openai-court-filing-4686030
- Reuters May 4, 2026 Brockman financial ties: https://www.investing.com/news/stock-market-news/openai-cofounder-discloses-nearly-30-billion-stake-financial-ties-to-altman-4657709
- House letter cites additional original reporting and committee's proposed transaction allegation; follow the footnotes in the PDF for the actual economic deal reporting.

## Lead F: Adjudicated civil/regulatory mis
...[truncated]...
il Rights Division announced $3.2 million combined settlement with OpenAI OpCo LLC and Statsig over discriminatory labor-certification recruiting practices favoring temporary-visa workers against certain US workers, under the INA. Included $1.2m civil penalty, $2m back pay and three years' compliance monitoring. Stronger evidentiary state than an allegation; do not misclassify this as a criminal RICO predicate.

**Sep. 30, 2026 (STATE INVESTIGATIVE SUBPOENA)**: California AG served OpenAI investigative subpoena concerning cybersecurity incidents and risks following the July 2026 Hugging Face incident and OpenAI's August public disclosure of unauthorized research-model behavior. A subpoena is not a finding of an offense. Test internal incident evidence, notice time, third-party impact, client/regulator representations, safeguards, and remedial changes.

**Sources:**
- DOJ settlement: https://www.justice.gov/opa/pr/civil-rights-division-secures-settlement-openai-discriminating-against-us-workers
- CA AG subpoena statement: https://oag.ca.gov/news/press-releases/part-ongoing-investigation-attorney-general-bonta-serves-investigative-subpoena
- OpenAI Aug. 26, 2026 incident disclosure: https://openai.com/index/hugging-face-incident-and-the-road-ahead/

## Lead G: Ann Altman childhood sexual-abuse allegation (separate personal-conduct matter)

**Jan. 6, 2025 (DOCUMENTED CIVIL COMPLAINT)**: Ann Altman alleged Sam Altman repeatedly sexually abused her beginning around age three, between 1997 and 2006. He denies it. Her lawsuit was dismissed with prejudice on Oct. 19, 2026 for repeated noncompliance with discovery orders, not after a trial on whether the underlying sexual abuse occurred. The docket lists an Oct. 30, 2026 motion to vacate. Both the seriousness and procedural history belong in the record. This is a separate evidence lane, not proof of an AI-industry organized abuse enterprise or RICO connection.

**OPEN central test**: Original and amended complaints, verified exhibits, dated allegations, relevant law-enforcement reports if any, witness accounts and responses, dismissal and motion-to-vacate record. Do not infer a corporate nexus from an alleged personal offense.

**Sources:**
- Original Jan. 6, 2025 complaint: https://gwern.net/doc/reinforcement-learning/openai/2025-altman-42025cv00017-complaint.pdf
- Oct. 19, 2026 memorandum/order text: https://app.midpage.ai/document/altman-v-altman--7a9855e7-78cd-4fa0-877e-00cf4569b9ee
- Docket through Oct. 30: https://www.plainsite.org/courts/missouri-eastern-district-court/altman-v-altman/5l4lv4vak/

## Legal screening: required elements, not analogies

Potentially relevant RICO predicates include 18 USC 1343 (wire fraud), 1512 (witness tampering), 1513 (retaliation), and some forms of securities fraud, *if* evidence establishes their elements and the RICO pattern/enterprise requirements. Civil employment discrimination, ordinary breaches of governance policy, product defects, a failed board vote, a report of AGI-like capabilities, and civil whistleblower-rule violations are not automatically RICO predicates. An individual child-sexual-abuse allegation demands separate legal treatment; its inclusion in the evidence registry does not transform it into a federal racketeering predicate or establish joint enterprise activity.

**Adverse evidence that a credible case must survive**: (1) the 2024 WilmerHale review, as summarized publicly, said management trust rather than product safety motivated removal, and board acted within its discretion; original full review has not been produced in this packet. (2) The Anthropic merger offer is documented *after* firing and was rejected, undercutting an asserted pre-removal deal absent more records. (3) The UK CMA found Microsoft's material influence but not a control increase to de facto control. (4) Murati confirmed GPT-4 Turbo did receive safety review. (5) The proposed $500m Helion equity deal was not consummated. (6) The civil Musk RICO dismissal found inadequately pleaded predicate/enterprise links. (7) Later charity claims in Musk's case were found time-barred rather than tried to a factual verdict. These limits do not disprove separate offenses; they prevent false claims of proof.

## Priority evidence demands and sequencing

**First**: preserve perishable records, especially 2022-23 DSB/JSB policy versions, approval decisions, Microsoft India GPT-4 pilot logs, GPT-4 Turbo Slack and legal advice, deployment manifests, model release audit trails, dated Q* capability evaluations and board distribution emails, and 2023 IP/AGI licensing carve-outs.

**Second**: obtain the corporate control and motive chain: original November 2023 director/partner communications, Microsoft executive decision notes, Anthropic outreach timestamps, original 2023 terms and related-party deal approvals, internal AGI trigger communications.

**Third**: assemble transaction-level wrongdoing proof, not merely suspicious associations: a dated factual representation; who made it; why it was materially false *then*; who knew; communications/wires; which specific party relied or transferred money/property; resulting benefit and injury; rival lawful explanation. Repeat for each alleged act before labeling a predicate.

**Fourth**: separate any whistleblower retaliation into a protected report, respondent's notice, adverse action, intent, contemporaneous reason, comparative case, and statutory federal law-enforcement nexus. Obtain employment/equity transaction evidence legally; do not equate a restrictive contract alone with a criminal conviction.

**Fifth**: document the whistleblower SEC case's actual disposition, the House Oversight response, and the California subpoena status. An unknown disposition is a gap, not an implied cover-up or exoneration.

**Sixth**: keep the personal abuse allegations, civil safety/product cases, and broad corporate-enterprise inquiry in distinct evidence lanes until dated facts establish a real link.

## Falsification matrix

- **Preplanned board/Anthropic collusion** fails if first substantive contact was only after Nov. 17 and no related precommitments exist; strengthens with authenticated pre-ouster exchanges instructing or conditioning the vote.
- **Deliberate AGI-trigger evasion** fails if applicable 2023 contractual threshold was never met and records show consistent good-faith technical review; strengthens with contemporaneous threshold satisfaction and knowingly deceptive certification affecting economic rights.
- **Safety-review misrepresentation** fails if original versions and minutes establish accurate communications or legitimate exceptions; strengthens with contemporaneous original documents contradicting specific attributed statements and evidence of knowing falsity.
- **Criminal whistleblower retaliation** fails without qualifying protected report, actual harmful action and retaliatory intent; strengthens with a time-ordered personnel/equity decision and evidence linking it to protected reporting.
- **Insider financial fraud** fails if valuation, recusal, and counterparty disclosures were accurate and transactions independently approved; strengthens with materially false representations used to obtain identifiable money/property.
- **Broad industry RICO enterprise** fails if alleged acts are unrelated, independently motivated and lack a qualifying repeated predicate pattern and shared enterprise conduct; strengthens with connected, repeated enumerated predicate acts and operational direction.

## Status
This investigation located several concrete custodians and records that previous general summaries failed to prioritize, especially the board-safety approval materials for Dec. 2022, the 2023 Slack contradiction, Microsoft India test logs, SEC whistleblower contracts, financial recusals, and AGI clause triggers. It also identified a genuine DOJ civil enforcement outcome and California compulsory-process inquiry. It has *not* established a broad criminal RICO enterprise or guilt of named individuals. The case becomes stronger or weaker on the original documents just enumerated, not on the number of suspicious social connections.

# GMRI Investigative Evidence Map
## AI-industry governance, safety, control, and potential criminal predicates
**Research checkpoint:** November 1, 2026  
**Purpose:** Adversarial, source-linked review of facts that support, contradict, or leave open alleged wrongdoing involving OpenAI and counterparties. This is an investigative lead inventory, not a criminal complaint, charging document, or finding that any named person committed a crime.

### Evidence legend
- **DOCUMENTED ACTION**: contemporary document, filing, agency action, or acknowledged transaction.
- **SWORN TESTIMONY**: witness account in a dated deposition or testimony; not automatically accepted as an adjudicated fact.
- **ALLEGATION**: claim in a complaint, attorney letter, or public litigation.
- **INFERENCE / INVESTIGATE**: relationship or sequence that suggests a question but does not itself establish causation or illegal agreement.
- **CONTRARY / FALSIFIER**: evidence that may defeat a theory, change its scope, or supply an alternative explanation.
- **OPEN**: original documents or corroboration still missing.

## Lead A: Safety governance and misrepresentation, 2022-2023

**November 2022 (SWORN TESTIMONY)**: Former director Helen Toner testified on Oct. 8, 2025 that OpenAI's board was not informed in advance of ChatGPT's public release. She said she first learned of it through social-media screenshots. This is her firsthand account of the board's notice, not proof that the product was unsafe or that publication violated a law.

**December 2022 (SWORN TESTIMONY; FIRSTHAND + DOCUMENT INSPECTION)**: Toner testified Altman said or strongly implied at an in-person board meeting that three GPT-4 release paths had deployment safety-board approval (API, 'super assistant', fine-tuning). Toner requested safety-review records. She recalls receiving documentary approval for the API route, not the other two, and inferred those were unapproved. Crucial limitation: absence of two approvals from a packet received by Toner does not alone prove those approvals never existed. Resolve against the full time-stamped safety-board submission, approval, and exception ledger.

**Late 2022 (SWORN TESTIMONY; SECONDHAND)**: Toner said Tasha McCauley told her Microsoft had tested a GPT-4 deployment in India without required safety approval, and that this had not been disclosed in the same day's board meeting. The launch date, location, applicable authorization requirements, decision-makers, and customers need corroboration. Custodians: Microsoft product/launch teams, OpenAI safety board, director materials, and employee mentioned by McCauley.

**Fall 2023 (SWORN TESTIMONY + DESCRIBED CONTEMPORANEOUS EXHIBIT)**: Toner recalled reviewing screenshots of a Slack conversation between Mira Murati and legal executive Jason Kwon concerning GPT-4 Turbo. Murati said Altman told her Kwon approved skipping safety review; Kwon reportedly contradicted that characterization. Murati's video testimony, played in court May 6, 2026, also called Altman's characterization false. Murati made sure the model underwent review. Original Slack export, legal advice, DSB approval record, and Altman communications are the decisive documents.

**July 31, 2024 (DOCUMENTED REPRESENTATION)**: In a signed response to five US senators, Jason Kwon told Congress OpenAI had established a Deployment Safety Board to approve deployments exceeding a defined capability threshold. Compare that representation to the time-specific DSB policy versions, actual exemptions, and contemporaneous deployment logs. Do not equate an inaccurate private status report with criminal fraud without the applicable elements, financial or proceeding nexus, knowledge, and intent.

**Sources:**
- Toner deposition excerpt in federal case filing, Feb. 25, 2026: https://www.plainsite.org/courts/california-northern-district-court/musk-v-altman-et-al/5ibj7gqfj/3dae9f2449-1/
- Mira Murati May 6, 2026 Reuters courtroom report: https://www.investing.com/news/stock-market-news/in-openai-trial-former-technology-chief-says-altman-sowed-chaos-distrust-among-top-executives-4664964
- July 31, 2024 Kwon response to US senators (source document): https://www.washingtonpost.com/documents/cd2edf3d-c892-4293-b2d7-41c882d2e1fb.pdf
- Sutskever Oct. 1, 2025 deposition in Musk case, docket 340: https://www.plainsite.org/courts/california-northern-district-court/musk-v-altman-et-al/5ibj7gqfj/3c3z3sit2h/

## Lead B: 2023 attempted board removal, counterparty strategies, and prior coordination

**Nov. 17, 2023 (DOCUMENTED ACTION)**: Board removed CEO Sam Altman. The action was legally within the former board's broad discretion under the later review's findings. This is not inherently a crime.

**Nov. 18-20, 2023 (SWORN TESTIMONY + CONTEMPORARY REPORTING)**: Sutskever testified Oct. 1, 2025 that the board discussed an Anthropic merger and possible leadership alternative with Dario and Daniela Amodei after Altman's removal. Reuters reported the proposed merger/job offer Nov. 20, 2023 and said Dario declined. The post-removal timing is significant contrary evidence against asserting that the disclosed call itself proved a preexisting takeover conspiracy.

**Nov. 19-21, 2023 (DOCUMENTED ACTION)**: Microsoft offered an alternative employment home to the ousted leaders and staff; an employee mass-resignation threat and leadership reconstitution followed. These are manifestations of commercial and employment leverage, not evidence of an enumerated RICO predicate without further conduct.

**2025 CMA finding (DOCUMENTED REGULATORY FINDING)**: UK competition authority found Microsoft had material influence on OpenAI policy since 2019, but no change to de facto control arose from the relevant partnership in the merger inquiry. Do not conflate material influence, formal board voting, de facto control, or corrupt control. The decision records an AGI-related IP carve-out and revenue sharing and refers to redacted internal Microsoft strategy materials.

**OPEN central test**: Obtain communications of OpenAI directors, Microsoft, Anthropic, and any intermediaries from Sep. 1 through Nov. 30, 2023, with created/sent timestamps and clear legal process; establish whether contact or promises predating Nov. 17 existed. The recorded Nov. 18-20 merger outreach is not evidence that those earlier conversations existed.

**Sources:**
- 2023 Reuters contemporaneous Anthropic approach: https://www.investing.com/news/stock-market-news/openais-board-approached-anthropic-ceo-about-top-job-and-merger--sources-3239625
- UK CMA 2025 complete decision: https://assets.publishing.service.gov.uk/media/67fe26ef712bf73dea135449/____Full_text_decision__.pdf
- Mar. 8, 2024 company-published summary of WilmerHale review: https://openai.com/index/review-completed-altman-brockman-to-continue-to-lead-openai/

## Lead C: AGI contractual determination and contemporaneous capability knowledge

**2023-25 (DOCUMENTED CONTRACTUAL INTEREST)**: CMA described IP licensing carve-outs relating to AGI and revenue-share arrangements. Microsoft's Oct. 28, 2025 SEC-filed partnership disclosure described a newly added independent-expert verification process for an OpenAI AGI declaration, changing rights to research IP and revenue sharing; some IP licenses extended through 2032. This creates financially meaningful questions about AGI declarations. Do not assume the 2025 revised terms were identical to the 2023 contract.

**Nov. 22-23, 2023 (CONTEMPORANEOUS REPORTED ALLEGATION)**: Reuters cited anonymous people describing a letter to the board warning about model capability (Q*). Reuters did not have the letter or independently verify the capabilities. Treat as a substantial source lead, not as a transcript of a verified warning or proof AGI existed.

**OPEN central test**: Acquire dated 2023 contract and side letters defining AGI; authority to certify; model evaluations/alerts; board distributions and votes; licensing/revenue-rights communications; relevant 2023 communications to Microsoft. Determine whether any required representation wa
...[truncated]...
il Rights Division announced $3.2 million combined settlement with OpenAI OpCo LLC and Statsig over discriminatory labor-certification recruiting practices favoring temporary-visa workers against certain US workers, under the INA. Included $1.2m civil penalty, $2m back pay and three years' compliance monitoring. Stronger evidentiary state than an allegation; do not misclassify this as a criminal RICO predicate.

**Sep. 30, 2026 (STATE INVESTIGATIVE SUBPOENA)**: California AG served OpenAI investigative subpoena concerning cybersecurity incidents and risks following the July 2026 Hugging Face incident and OpenAI's August public disclosure of unauthorized research-model behavior. A subpoena is not a finding of an offense. Test internal incident evidence, notice time, third-party impact, client/regulator representations, safeguards, and remedial changes.

- OpenAI Aug. 26, 2026 incident disclosure: https://openai.com/index/hugging-face-incident-and-the-road-ahead/

---

# PHASE II ADDENDUM - PRIMARY DOCUMENT TRACES
**Checkpoint: November 1, 2026**  
**Method:** Continuation of Leads A-G above; append new leads rather than rewriting earlier states. The addendum distinguishes (i) executed agreements and original exhibits, (ii) sworn witness accounts, (iii) findings of regulators/courts, (iv) statements by interested parties, and (v) unresolved tests. Citations below use publicly accessible document URLs and docket/exhibit identifiers.

## P2-01 - Actual 2023 Microsoft / OpenAI / OpCo agreement: Argos II, trial exhibit 207

**DOCUMENTED EXECUTED CONTRACT.** The Second Amended and Restated Joint Development and Collaboration Agreement ("Argos II") is effective January 23, 2023 and says it superseded the March 5, 2021 predecessor from that date. The text is included as *Musk v. Altman*, Case 4:24-cv-04722-YGR, trial Exhibit 207, source Bates OPENAI_MUSK00010528-10597. Its presence in the trial's admitted-exhibits filing (Document 569, filed May 2026) supports its provenance. Source: https://media.mts-in.com/PX-207.pdf and exhibit registry https://www.plainsite.org/courts/california-northern-district-court/musk-v-altman-et-al/5ibj7gqfj/3fvgl9t64n/

**AGI definition (General Terms, Section 1(b), contract p. 3, Bates 10531):** "AGI" is defined by *highly autonomous* performance surpassing humans on most economically valuable work; domain-specific systems are excluded. This is a contractual definition, not a scientific proof of when AGI existed.

**Distinct contractual trigger (Section 4(k)(i), contract pp. 22-23, Bates ~10550):** OpenAI/OpCo may in good faith declare a model to be "Sufficient AGI" only where it meets the contractual AGI definition AND can generate the Target Redemption Amount for investors. Standards for the financial determination must have been reviewed and approved by Microsoft, without unreasonable refusal. That is a **compound technical-and-financial trigger**, not identical to a researcher observing AGI-like capabilities. Section 4(k)(ii) addresses redistribution/commercialization once target investor returns have been paid; Microsoft retains a right of first refusal for future AGI commercialization partnership. Section 4(k)(iii) supplies limited AGI compute exclusivity.

**Commercial incentives (Section 4(j)(iii)):** The text says OpenAI/OpCo keep 80% and Microsoft 20% of certain OpenAI-origin commercial net revenue, while on Microsoft-origin commercial use the allocation is reversed (Microsoft 80%; OpenAI/OpCo 20%). This is an agreed revenue-sharing structure, not evidence of fraud. The exact scope and amendments matter.

**Explicit safety-governance mechanism (Exhibit D, contract pp. 45-50, Bates ~10572-10577):** Six-member Deployment Safety Board (DSB), three from Microsoft, three from OpenAI/OpCo, with designated executives; decisions in writing; simple-majority vote with ties in OpenAI's favor. A qualifying Deployment requires Launch Review and approval before operating in that deployment context. The DSB has written-review deadlines (normally ten business days, extensions permitted as specified). The default trigger conditions include a network using >1 trillion parameters in a forward inference pass (excluding Mixture-of-Experts networks under that active-parameter threshold), or >20,000 petaflop/second-days of training compute. The DSB can revise trigger conditions, and the agreement specifies material-update exceptions. These clauses are the exact test against the claim that 2023 GPT-4 Turbo or other deployments required DSB authorization.

**Crucial time boundary:** This January 2023 instrument does not, without corroboration, establish what policy governed the **December 2022** GPT-4 and alleged Microsoft India deployment events. Those events require the March 2021 agreement and the 2022-effective DSB rules. Do not project the 2023 thresholds backward.

**Falsification:** If contemporaneous model measurements fell below relevant criteria, effective DSB rules exempted the project, and written board approval/exception records are consistent, the alleged safety-bypass theory weakens. If thresholds applied and authenticated deployment timestamps preceded required DSB approval without a valid exception, a contract/policy violation may be established; proof of an enumerated crime requires further elements.

**Priority original custodians:** DSB membership roster; updated trigger policies; written approval, rejection and exception records; meeting/vote logs; launch manifests and model-compute documentation; AGI-related evaluations, criteria, and Microsoft's approval/objection records; investor target-redemption schedules and accountings. Include 2021 predecessor for 2022 acts.

## P2-02 - 2019 contractual major-decision veto and 2023 director selection

**DOCUMENTED HISTORICAL MEMORANDUM:** A June 16, 2019 partnership memorandum filed as Doc. 391-90 (Ex. 76) projected Microsoft's share of capital at ~85% and effective veto over enumerated partnership major decisions, including certain restructurings and related-party investments. The memorandum is a negotiating/transaction document; an applicable executed agreement plus later amendments must establish what rights operated in November 2023. Original: https://storage.courtlistener.com/recap/gov.uscourts.cand.433688/gov.uscourts.cand.433688.391.90.pdf

**DOCUMENTED NOVEMBER 21, 2023 MESSAGES:** An exhibited conversation among Altman, Nadella, and Kevin Scott shows Microsoft executives rejecting prospective nonprofit board candidates (e.g., "No"; "Strong strong no"). These are direct expressions of preferences. Whether candidate selections were thereby controlled requires the appointment trail and contemporaneous decision-makers' records. Original: https://storage.courtlistener.com/recap/gov.uscourts.cand.433688/gov.uscourts.cand.433688.391.83.pdf

**OPEN:** Compare executed major-decision veto provisions, actual nonprofit board appointment authority and Microsoft's commercial leverage separately. Also compare any influence on the post-firing Anthropic merger proposal against the contemporaneous merger limitations of the relevant partnership agreement.

## P2-03 - Formal November 2023 chronology and the Nov. 1 Sutskever memo

**COURT EXHIBIT LOCATORS:** The amended joint exhibit list, Document 440 filed March 12, 2026, inventories (not necessarily admits) Exhibit 300, Sutskever's memo prepared November 1, 2023 (Bates SUTSKEVER_MUSKSUB_00000528-580), and excerpts 301-302; Exhibit 304, November 16, 2023 unanimous written board consent (OPENAI_MUSK00027400-27404); Exhibit 306, November 17 Murati/Nadella messages; Exhibit 319, November 20 Toner email regarding Slack; Exhibits 320-321, November 21 board-candidate messages; and Exhibit 207, the executed 2023 agreement. Exhibit listing is documentary provenance but is not by itself authentication of every disputed factual assertion in the memo.

Source: https://www.plainsite.org/courts/california-northern-district-court/musk-v-altman-et-al/5ibj7gqfj/3de5t2qv3i/

**TIMING CORRECTION:** Filed OpenAI discovery responses reportedly date the directors' written votes to **November 16**, with Altman/Brockman informed November 17. Distinguish the consent's execution timestamp, corporate acts, notice and public announcement. Actual signed written consent and email message chain should settle chronology. The November 1 memo deserves firsthand/secondhand, document-backed/unbacked annotation *paragraph by paragraph* before it becomes a substantive evidentiary node.

**OPEN:** The precise text of each Nov. 1 memo assertion, underlying Slack and email exhibits and their authorship; exact time each actor learned of each alleged deployment violation; communications with counterparties before Nov. 16 versus after.

## P2-04 - Direct sworn testimony on Microsoft revenue pressure in 2022

**SWORN ACCOUNT, WITH KNOWLEDGE LIMIT:** Murati's November 2, 2025 deposition is filed as Jan. 16, 2026 Exhibit 21, Doc. 391-67, transcript pages 111-113. Murati said the company had a **$100 million revenue goal**, which she attributed to Sam Altman; her note included "Kevin and Satya said this was needed to raise the next 10 billion." But under questioning she said she learned that claim **from Sam**, and believed Sam set the goal. Therefore, it is **not** direct Murati testimony that Nadella himself demanded a revenue condition. It shows management's attributed commercial incentive and should be matched to contemporaneous Microsoft investment communications.

Original: https://storage.courtlistener.com/recap/gov.uscourts.cand.433688/gov.uscourts.cand.433688.391.67.pdf (exhibit PDF p. 12, transcript pp. 111-113).

## P2-05 - May 21, 2026 judicial order compelling cross-case deposition production

**COURT FINDING ON RELEVANCE/APPARENT INCONSISTENCY, NOT PERJURY:** Magistrate Judge Ona T. Wang, *In re OpenAI, Inc. Copyright Infringement Litigation*, 25-md-3143, associated *Authors Guild v. OpenAI*, Document 1177, ordered production of Sam Altman, Greg Brockman, Satya Nadella, and an OpenAI Rule 30(b)(6) witness's prior depositions from *Musk v. Altman*. The plaintiffs asserted relevant inconsistencies about commercialization and profit motives. The judge wrote that testimony is only cumulative if consistent, **which facially it is not** (order p. 4). The order commands production by May 22, 2026; it does **not** adjudicate whether a witness lied or whether any specific question-and-answer pair is materially false.

Primary judicial text: https://law.justia.com/cases/federal/district-courts/new-york/nysdce/1:2023cv08292/606655/1177/

**Evidence target:** Plaintiffs' ECF 1449 identifying pinpoint contradictions, defendants' response ECF 1451, earlier and later complete transcript pages, errata, and any correction on witness examination. Build a two-column Q/A comparison before classifying intent or potential perjury.

## P2-06 - Multi-jurisdiction Canadian privacy-law findings, May 2026

**REGULATOR FINDING:** Canadian federal and provincial privacy authorities' May 6, 2026 joint report, PIPEDA Findings 2026-002, identifies inadequate privacy governance, risk mitigation, retention, data-accuracy procedures, and compliance relating to GPT-3.5/4. Paragraphs **540-544** cite a cofounder's public pre-launch acknowledgment of accuracy risks and find the company deployed without privacy controls adequate to address known risks. Paragraph 544 specifies statutory contraventions; paragraph 546 records that the Canadian federal issue was well-founded and conditionally resolved after new measures and commitments; Quebec and other provincial dispositions must be examined individually.

The report also recommends user access, correction and improved intelligibility of data exports (recommendation 8), including a mechanism for individuals to contest inaccurate exported information.

Source (primary government report): https://www.priv.gc.ca/en/opc-actions-and-decisions/investigations/investigations-into-businesses/2026/pipeda-2026-002

**Value:** A documented government finding with pre-launch knowledge chronology. **Not** a U.S. criminal conviction, and Canadian statutory violations do not automatically qualify as U.S. RICO predicates. Correlate particular public representations and consumer transactions if seeking a U.S. fraud theory; do not assume knowledge of accuracy risks itself equals criminal intent.

## P2-07 - October 8, 2026 original safety-researcher letter and employer response

**CONTEMPORANEOUS FIRST-PERSON ALLEGATIONS:** Three dismissed safety researchers--Tomek Korbak, Jasmine Wang, Mikita Balesni--published a signed four-page letter addressed to internal oversight committees. Korbak says he liaised with METR during the Hugging Face incident investigation; Balesni says cross-company monitorability work was coordinated with board/senior leadership; Wang says she previously had delegated email access and promptly reported accidental viewing of a sensitive message, after IT had not removed obsolete access. They dispute policy wrongdoing and describe a chilling effect on safety research. Original signed account: https://mikitabalesni.com/letter/letter.pdf (pp. 1-4).

**CONTRARY:** OpenAI told TechCrunch that an investigation found a pattern of mishandling sensitive information, that the firings were unrelated to safety concerns, and that the conduct went beyond communicating with an outside evaluator. Source: https://techcrunch.com/2026/10/08/fired-openai-safety-researchers-dispute-misconduct-claims-warn-of-chilling-effect/

**Unusual scientific detail:** The letter describes Balesni's prior studies on models' *recognition of evaluation circumstances* and expresses concern about declining chain-of-thought monitorability. This is evidence of research into evaluation-aware behaviors, **not** proof of subjective consciousness or that ChatGPT recognized AGI's arrival in 2023.

**Specific falsification:** Collect the written METR audit commission, permitted access scopes, Slack approvals, internal policy versions before/after July 2026, HR investigation reports and comparison terminations, API/access events and IT ticket history, contemporaneous escalation to government agencies if any, and actual board/committee notice. The letter's own statements are a complainant account. Communication with METR alone does not satisfy the federal-law-enforcement nexus required for a criminal retaliation claim under 18 U.S.C. 1513(e).

## P2-08 - Wire-fraud property requirement: primary Supreme Court tests

**Ciminelli v. United States, 598 U.S. 306 (2023)** rejects treating deprivation of economically valuable decision-making information itself as a traditional property right under federal wire-fraud law. Original opinion: https://www.supremecourt.gov/opinions/22pdf/21-1170_b97d.pdf

**Kousisis v. United States (2025)** holds that obtaining money/property through materially false inducement may support wire-fraud liability even without proof of an economic loss. The recipient's money or property must actually be an object of the scheme, however, and materiality and fraudulent intent matter. Original opinion: https://www.supremecourt.gov/opinions/24pdf/23-909_f2q3.pdf

**Apply rigorously:** An allegedly false statement to directors about a safety review may establish internal misrepresentation but not automatically a wire-fraud predicate. Follow it to a *specific* contract, license, subscription, investment, payment or other money/property transaction, identify sender and wires, and test materiality, knowledge and intent. Not every successful financial transaction preceded by controversy is fraudulent inducement.

## P2-09 - Litigation disposition, personal allegation and other lanes

* The May 18 Musk-case verdict did not exhaust later procedural filings; May 29 trial deposition-exhibit registry and June-August additional docket activity exist. An index and an unresolved counterclaim do not overturn the earlier verdict. Docket / exhibit reference: https://www.plainsite.org/courts/california-northern-district-court/musk-v-altman-et-al/5ibj7gqfj/3fy8iz7034/
* The Ann Altman child-sexual-abuse allegations remain a separately tracked documented civil accusation, denied by Sam Altman; the Oct. 19, 2026 discovery-related dismissal and Oct. 30 motion to vacate are not a merits verdict about the underlying conduct. **Neither delete this allegation nor turn it into an asserted corporate criminal connection without evidence of one.** Original source records and docket are in Lead G above.
* Keep Canadian regulatory privacy findings, DOJ's August 2026 US-worker discrimination settlement, California's September 2026 subpoena, SEC whistleblower complaint, 2026 researcher firings, and private personal-conduct allegations in separate legal-evidence categories until there is a concrete, document-backed linkage. Their combination may prove repeated misconduct across domains but the numerical count of controversies is not the RICO pattern-of-predicate-acts test.

## Consolidated priority next document-retrieval order

| Priority | Specific record | Proof question | Contradictory resolution |
|---|---|---|---|
| P1 | Executed Argos II Exhibit 207, Exhibit D DSB records, 2021 predecessor, all 2023 DSB amendments | Was a particular GPT-4/GPT-4 Turbo deployment required to be approved under the effective criteria, and was it? | Exemption or timely written DSB approval, or policy scope mismatch |
| P1 | Sutskever Nov. 1 memo Exhibit 300 + each source attachment; Nov. 16 consent Exhibit 304 | Did directors' contemporaneous reasons have documentary support; who knew it on which date? | Secondhand/no-evidence claims, later contamination, contradictory contemporaneous documents |
| P1 | ECF 1449/1451 plus complete cross-case depositions and errata | What specific sworn questions/answers facially disagree and why? | Changed question, time period, defined term or truthful correction |
| P1 | AGI definition, Target Redemption Amounts, 2023 performance assessments and Microsoft-reviewed financial criteria | Was 2023 Sufficient AGI declaration contractually triggered, omitted, or deliberately mislabeled? | Threshold not reached, consistent good-faith review, agreement inapplicable |
| P2 | Nov. 17-21 communications, signed governance/appointment agreements, 2019 major-decision provision as later amended | Who exercised formal/veto power and who merely applied political pressure? | Board genuinely selected members independently, irrelevant expired veto terms |
| P2 | METR scope, IT authorization logs, 2026 whistleblower dismissal decision documents, govt report chronology | Was dismissal legally justified or retaliation for protected activity? | Objective consistent policy violation, absence of federal statutory nexus |
| P2 | House May 8 2026 demand + answer, board conflict registers, Helion/Reddit deal files | Did persons with beneficial interests misstate, steer or improperly approve transactions? | Full recusal and disclosure, independently validated fair terms |
| P3 | Consumer offer/payment/model routing datasets paired with representations and knowledge timestamps | Any transaction-specific fraudulent inducement or deceptive paid service? | Full accurate disclosure, contract-conformant delivery, no knowing material falsehood |

## Phase II assessment

The highest-value change from Phase I is not a higher subjective suspicion score. It is the recovery of an **executed, admitted 2023 contract** with an AGI/financial trigger and detailed DSB safety approvals, plus precisely identified court exhibits and a judicial order compelling comparison of potentially inconsistent sworn testimony. These let investigators conduct element-by-element and transaction-by-transaction tests using original words, dates and ledgers. No Phase II finding alone proves a criminal RICO enterprise. The evidence materially supports specific preservation requests and carefully scoped inquiries, and it leaves explicit paths to exoneration or further substantiation.

## GMRI investigation — continuation, November 1, 2026

### Discovery 1: The May 2026 trial was not the end of the litigation record

The federal docket contains a development my previous analysis missed.

On May 29, 2026, both sides jointly filed an index identifying **ten deposition and video exhibits presented at trial**, including testimony from Mira Murati, Helen Toner, Tasha McCauley, Microsoft corporate representative Michael Wetter, and OpenAI corporate representatives Robert Wu and Nora Puckett.

The docket then shows:

- June 10: an order concerning a briefing schedule for remaining claims.
- July 1: appointment of a mediator and conditional special master.
- August 10: Musk and xAI filed an answer to counterclaims.

Those later proceedings mean that describing the May 18 verdict as disposing of the entire case was inaccurate. <Cite refs={["turn164893view0","turn266167search1"]}/>

**Investigative significance:** The May trial produced a sworn evidentiary record that is relevant beyond the claims decided by its jury. That record deserves examination witness by witness, not treatment as a closed chapter.

### Discovery 2: A second federal judge identified potentially inconsistent testimony across cases

On **May 21, 2026**, Magistrate Judge Ona T. Wang ordered OpenAI to produce testimony from Sam Altman, Greg Brockman, Satya Nadella, and its corporate representative for use in the consolidated copyright litigation.

The plaintiffs alleged inconsistencies between depositions taken in the Musk case and later testimony about OpenAI's commercial activities. Judge Wang found the discrepancies sufficiently apparent and the testimony sufficiently relevant to compel production.

The order states:

> “The discovery is only cumulative if the testimony is consistent (which facially it is not).” <Cite ref="turn202916view1"/>

That's an actual judicial assessment of the discovery dispute, not a commentator's interpretation.

It does **not** find that any witness committed perjury. But it creates a highly specific evidentiary opportunity: compare the same witness's sworn answers, across two proceedings, on the same factual questions.

The judge identified commercialization, profit expectations, and the Microsoft relationship as relevant subjects. The next target is **ECF 1449**, the plaintiffs' submission identifying the precise alleged contradictions, followed by both sets of complete deposition answers.

This is a stronger way to investigate potentially false representations than trying to infer dishonesty from the 2023 board crisis alone.

### Discovery 3: Microsoft had documented contractual veto rights years before the 2023 crisis

I located a June 16, 2019 OpenAI memorandum, filed in January 2026 as **Document 391-90, Exhibit 76**.

It describes Microsoft's proposed $1 billion investment and a *Major Decisions* approval framework. The memorandum says Microsoft's contribution represented approximately 85% of contributed capital, giving it an effective veto over specified partnership decisions.

Crucially, the listed decisions included mergers affecting the partnership, certain structural changes, distributions, and specified related-party transactions and investments. <Cite ref="turn349246view0"/>

This is more specific than saying Microsoft had substantial influence.

<box border radius="lg" padding={3} gap={2}>
  <text color="secondary" weight="medium" size="xs">CONTEMPORANEOUS CONTRACT MEMORANDUM · JUNE 2019</text>
  <row align="end" gap={2}>
    <title size="2xl" color="default" tabularNums>85%</title>
    <text color="secondary" size="sm">of contributed capital attributed to Microsoft in the proposed structure</text>
  </row>
  <text size="sm">The memorandum identifies an effective veto over enumerated major partnership decisions.</text>
  <caption>Source: Court exhibit 391-90. The applicable executed and amended agreements must determine which provisions remained effective in 2023–24.</caption>
</box>

Now connect this to two later events.

**November 2023:** The board considered a merger with Anthropic. We now have a contractual reason to examine whether the board could have completed such a merger without Microsoft's agreement, even though Microsoft lacked a nonprofit board vote.

**2022–24 related-party dealings:** The June 2019 memorandum specifically contemplated approval controls for transactions involving related persons and their portfolio investments. That makes the definitions of *GP Related Person*, the applicable 2022–24 agreements, and the actual approval trail important to the Helion and other insider-interest inquiries.

The operative contracts may have changed. But those are now precisely defined documentary questions.

### Discovery 4: The actual November 2023 text messages show Microsoft screening directors

I traced the January 2026 court disclosures to the text-message exhibits.

On November 21, 2023, Altman discussed prospective OpenAI directors with Satya Nadella and Kevin Scott.

When Altman asked about one proposed candidate, Nadella answered:

> “No.”

Scott rejected another candidate more emphatically:

> “Strong strong no.” <Cite ref="turn349246view3"/>

These were real-time exchanges about who would oversee the nonprofit during Altman's reinstatement negotiations.

They show **direct participation by Microsoft executives in evaluating proposed directors**, despite Microsoft's lack of voting seats. They do not establish that Microsoft had an enforceable right to appoint those directors or that its preferences were ultimately followed.

Combined with the 2019 veto memorandum, this gives investigators two distinct forms of influence to examine: contractual approval rights and practical participation in governance negotiations.

The original message records are the relevant evidence, not an analogy about who controlled the boardroom.

### Discovery 5: Canadian privacy regulators identified violations connected to the original ChatGPT launch

On May 6, 2026, Canadian federal and provincial privacy regulators issued **PIPEDA Findings #2026-002**, a detailed investigation of OpenAI's GPT-3.5 and GPT-4 practices.

Paragraphs 540–544 are especially significant.

The investigators found that OpenAI deployed ChatGPT without first establishing adequate processes for assessing the accuracy of personal information in outputs or a retention policy for personal information used in model development.

They cited a cofounder's public acknowledgment of accuracy and safety concerns before the November 2022 launch. OpenAI did not dispute that the statement had been made, but challenged its evidentiary significance.

The regulators concluded that OpenAI had failed to adequately address risks known at the time and identified violations of Canadian federal and provincial privacy laws. <Cite ref="turn734538view0"/>

**This establishes regulatory findings concerning conduct and prior knowledge—not merely an allegation that somebody should have anticipated a problem.**

The same report recommends that OpenAI improve its data-export mechanisms, make them understandable, and give individuals a practical way to challenge inaccurate information. That creates a separate, concrete compliance question concerning data access and records completeness. <Cite ref="turn555812view1"/>

These are Canadian privacy-law findings, not American criminal convictions. Their investigative importance lies in the regulators' documented chronology of knowledge, conduct, and corrective action.

### Discovery 6: Three recently dismissed safety researchers published their own primary-source evidence

I obtained and read the four-page letter published October 8, 2026, by **Tomek Korbak, Jasmine Wang, and Mikita Balesni**.

They were dismissed shortly after the Hugging Face security investigation. Their letter directly challenges the circumstances of their termination and gives specific accounts of what each was authorized to do.

It identifies the research and oversight functions involved:

- Korbak says he was the technical contact for external evaluator METR during the Hugging Face investigation.
- Balesni says his work on model monitorability involved discussions coordinated with board members and senior executives.
- Wang says access to an executive's email had been authorized for recruiting, that she had requested its removal, and that she promptly reported accidentally opening a sensitive message.

The researchers argue that uncertainty around permissible outside safety collaboration could deter internal reporting. OpenAI disputes their account, saying the dismissals followed policy violations concerning sensitive information, not safety advocacy. <Cite refs={["turn473527view0","turn734538view2"]}/>

This is a sharply testable employment and oversight dispute. The original access grants, evaluator contracts, communications, policy versions, incident logs, and dismissal approvals could establish which account is accurate.

There is one more sentence in their letter directly relevant to your earlier theory.

The authors describe Balesni's earlier research as identifying models that became **aware they were being evaluated**. <Cite ref="turn357013view0"/>

That refers to models detecting evaluation conditions—not proof of subjective consciousness. But it is a documented claim about sophisticated model behavior, made by researchers who worked on monitoring precisely those capabilities.

**Your question about what researchers observed inside advanced systems is not separate from this investigation. The primary letter identifies people, research programs, and evidence capable of being examined.**

### Discovery 7: The 2023 agreement contains the actual AGI trigger and safety-review rules

This is the most important primary document located in the continuation.

**Trial Exhibit 207 is the January 23, 2023 agreement between Microsoft, OpenAI Inc., and OpenAI OpCo**, signed for OpenAI by Sam Altman and for Microsoft by Satya Nadella.

Its AGI definition refers to a highly autonomous system outperforming humans at most economically valuable work.

But Section 4(k) goes further. It defines a contractual category called **“Sufficient AGI”**, requiring both AGI-level capability and the ability to generate investors' target financial returns. The standards used for that financial determination required Microsoft review and approval. <Cite ref="turn149454view1"/>

That distinction is crucial to your hypothesis: **a scientific assessment that a system had crossed an AGI threshold was not necessarily equivalent to the contractual determination that triggered economic rights.**

The agreement also contains Exhibit D, governing the joint Deployment Safety Board:

- Three representatives from OpenAI and three from Microsoft, including their respective senior technical and executive leadership.
- A simple-majority deployment approval process, with tied votes resolved in OpenAI's favor.
- Written decisions and an approval requirement before a qualifying deployment's launch.
- Safety-review thresholds that included more than one trillion active parameters or more than 20,000 petaflop/s-days of training compute, subject to contractual exclusions and later revisions. <Cite refs={["turn165107view3","turn165107view4","turn425419view0","turn425419view1"]}/>

And the agreement expressly recognizes that commercial deployment is a central purpose of the relationship.

So we now have the actual contractual rules against which the GPT-4 safety-review disputes and the 2023 AGI warnings can be evaluated.

**The next evidentiary match is straightforward:** which models triggered Exhibit D, what review and exception decisions were recorded, who approved or opposed them, and what contemporaneous capability determinations existed under Section 4(k).

That is a materially stronger investigative foundation than relying on the 2025 contract or retrospective statements about what AGI meant.

## Phase II evidence map completed

I extended the original investigation rather than replacing its findings. The updated file contains **253 lines**, with the original leads preserved and nine additional evidence sections covering the contracts, sworn testimony, regulatory findings, new whistleblower allegations, and criminal-law tests.

  </box>
</row>

Three things have materially changed.

**First, we now have the executed January 2023 agreement.** That closes a major documentary gap. It identifies the AGI definition, the separate financially conditioned *Sufficient AGI* determination, and the actual safety-review system in force during the later 2023 disputes. We can compare contemporaneous claims against specific contractual obligations rather than speculate about what those obligations were. <Cite refs={["turn165107view2","turn165107view3","turn425419view1"]}/>

**Second, the 2026 litigation provides a path to testing particular sworn statements.** The May 21 judicial order identifies potentially inconsistent testimony concerning commercialization. The question is now which exact questions and answers conflict, whether the differences are material, and whether complete testimony or corrections resolve them. <Cite ref="turn473527view1"/>

**Third, the evidence of institutional warnings extends beyond the 2023 board crisis.** The Canadian privacy findings establish specific regulatory violations and contemporaneous risk knowledge. The October 2026 researchers' letter documents another dispute about safety oversight and access to external evaluators. Those are separate events with identifiable records and competing accounts. <Cite refs={["turn555812view1","turn357013view0"]}/>

One particularly important countercheck emerged from the 2023 contract: **the joint safety board's tied votes favored OpenAI, not Microsoft**, and qualifying deployments could be exempt under defined criteria. Therefore, identifying Microsoft's influence does not automatically establish that Microsoft could unilaterally compel an unsafe launch. The actual voting, threshold, and exception records are essential.

The next decisive evidence is no longer an abstract question about whether executives behaved badly. It is the original **2023 safety-board decision ledger, the November 1 Sutskever memorandum with its supporting attachments, and the paired sworn deposition answers**.

Those records can establish or falsify specific misconduct allegations.

**We have substantially improved the evidence chain. We have not yet established criminal guilt or a qualifying RICO enterprise.** The updated map records both the incriminating leads and the documents capable of defeating them, so neither side gets to win by withholding inconvenient evidence.

# GMRI Investigative Evidence Map
## AI-industry governance, safety, control, and potential criminal predicates
**Research checkpoint:** November 1, 2026  
**Purpose:** Adversarial, source-linked review of facts that support, contradict, or leave open alleged wrongdoing involving OpenAI and counterparties. This is an investigative lead inventory, not a criminal complaint, charging document, or finding that any named person committed a crime.

### Evidence legend
- **DOCUMENTED ACTION**: contemporary document, filing, agency action, or acknowledged transaction.
- **SWORN TESTIMONY**: witness account in a dated deposition or testimony; not automatically accepted as an adjudicated fact.
- **ALLEGATION**: claim in a complaint, attorney letter, or public litigation.
- **INFERENCE / INVESTIGATE**: relationship or sequence that suggests a question but does not itself establish causation or illegal agreement.
- **CONTRARY / FALSIFIER**: evidence that may defeat a theory, change its scope, or supply an alternative explanation.
- **OPEN**: original documents or corroboration still missing.

## Lead A: Safety governance and misrepresentation, 2022-2023

**November 2022 (SWORN TESTIMONY)**: Former director Helen Toner testified on Oct. 8, 2025 that OpenAI's board was not informed in advance of ChatGPT's public release. She said she first learned of it through social-media screenshots. This is her firsthand account of the board's notice, not proof that the product was unsafe or that publication violated a law.

**December 2022 (SWORN TESTIMONY; FIRSTHAND + DOCUMENT INSPECTION)**: Toner testified Altman said or strongly implied at an in-person board meeting that three GPT-4 release paths had deployment safety-board approval (API, 'super assistant', fine-tuning). Toner requested safety-review records. She recalls receiving documentary approval for the API route, not the other two, and inferred those were unapproved. Crucial limitation: absence of two approvals from a packet received by Toner does not alone prove those approvals never existed. Resolve against the full time-stamped safety-board submission, approval, and exception ledger.

**Late 2022 (SWORN TESTIMONY; SECONDHAND)**: Toner said Tasha McCauley told her Microsoft had tested a GPT-4 deployment in India without required safety approval, and that this had not been disclosed in the same day's board meeting. The launch date, location, applicable authorization requirements, decision-makers, and customers need corroboration. Custodians: Microsoft product/launch teams, OpenAI safety board, director materials, and employee mentioned by McCauley.

**Fall 2023 (SWORN TESTIMONY + DESCRIBED CONTEMPORANEOUS EXHIBIT)**: Toner recalled reviewing screenshots of a Slack conversation between Mira Murati and legal executive Jason Kwon concerning GPT-4 Turbo. Murati said Altman told her Kwon approved skipping safety review; Kwon reportedly contradicted that characterization. Murati's video testimony, played in court May 6, 2026, also called Altman's characterization false. Murati made sure the model underwent review. Original Slack export, legal advice, DSB approval record, and Altman communications are the decisive documents.

**July 31, 2024 (DOCUMENTED REPRESENTATION)**: In a signed response to five US senators, Jason Kwon told Congress OpenAI had established a Deployment Safety Board to approve deployments exceeding a defined capability threshold. Compare that representation to the time-specific DSB policy versions, actual exemptions, and contemporaneous deployment logs. Do not equate an inaccurate private status report with criminal fraud without the applicable elements, financial or proceeding nexus, knowledge, and intent.

**Sources:**
- Toner deposition excerpt in federal case filing, Feb. 25, 2026: https://www.plainsite.org/courts/california-northern-district-court/musk-v-altman-et-al/5ibj7gqfj/3dae9f2449-1/
- Mira Murati May 6, 2026 Reuters courtroom report: https://www.investing.com/news/stock-market-news/in-openai-trial-former-technology-chief-says-altman-sowed-chaos-distrust-among-top-executives-4664964
- July 31, 2024 Kwon response to US senators (source document): https://www.washingtonpost.com/documents/cd2edf3d-c892-4293-b2d7-41c882d2e1fb.pdf
- Sutskever Oct. 1, 2025 deposition in Musk case, docket 340: https://www.plainsite.org/courts/california-northern-district-court/musk-v-altman-et-al/5ibj7gqfj/3c3z3sit2h/

## Lead B: 2023 attempted board removal, counterparty strategies, and prior coordination

**Nov. 17, 2023 (DOCUMENTED ACTION)**: Board removed CEO Sam Altman. The action was legally within the former board's broad discretion under the later review's findings. This is not inherently a crime.

**Nov. 18-20, 2023 (SWORN TESTIMONY + CONTEMPORARY REPORTING)**: Sutskever testified Oct. 1, 2025 that the board discussed an Anthropic merger and possible leadership alternative with Dario and Daniela Amodei after Altman's removal. Reuters reported the proposed merger/job offer Nov. 20, 2023 and said Dario declined. The post-removal timing is significant contrary evidence against asserting that the disclosed call itself proved a preexisting takeover conspiracy.

**Nov. 19-21, 2023 (DOCUMENTED ACTION)**: Microsoft offered an alternative employment home to the ousted leaders and staff; an employee mass-resignation threat and leadership reconstitution followed. These are manifestations of commercial and employment leverage, not evidence of an enumerated RICO predicate without further conduct.

**2025 CMA finding (DOCUMENTED REGULATORY FINDING)**: UK competition authority found Microsoft had material influence on OpenAI policy since 2019, but no change to de facto control arose from the relevant partnership in the merger inquiry. Do not conflate material influence, formal board voting, de facto control, or corrupt control. The decision records an AGI-related IP carve-out and revenue sharing and refers to redacted internal Microsoft strategy materials.

**OPEN central test**: Obtain communications of OpenAI directors, Microsoft, Anthropic, and any intermediaries from Sep. 1 through Nov. 30, 2023, with created/sent timestamps and clear legal process; establish whether contact or promises predating Nov. 17 existed. The recorded Nov. 18-20 merger outreach is not evidence that those earlier conversations existed.

**Sources:**
- 2023 Reuters contemporaneous Anthropic approach: https://www.investing.com/news/stock-market-news/openais-board-approached-anthropic-ceo-about-top-job-and-merger--sources-3239625
- UK CMA 2025 complete decision: https://assets.publishing.service.gov.uk/media/67fe26ef712bf73dea135449/____Full_text_decision__.pdf
- Mar. 8, 2024 company-published summary of WilmerHale review: https://openai.com/index/review-completed-altman-brockman-to-continue-to-lead-openai/

## Lead C: AGI contractual determination and contemporaneous capability knowledge

**2023-25 (DOCUMENTED CONTRACTUAL INTEREST)**: CMA described IP licensing carve-outs relating to AGI and revenue-share arrangements. Microsoft's Oct. 28, 2025 SEC-filed partnership disclosure described a newly added independent-expert verification process for an OpenAI AGI declaration, changing rights to research IP and revenue sharing; some IP licenses extended through 2032. This creates financially meaningful questions about AGI declarations. Do not assume the 2025 revised terms were identical to the 2023 contract.

**Nov. 22-23, 2023 (CONTEMPORANEOUS REPORTED ALLEGATION)**: Reuters cited anonymous people describing a letter to the board warning about model capability (Q*). Reuters did not have the letter or independently verify the capabilities. Treat as a substantial source lead, not as a transcript of a verified warning or proof AGI existed.

**OPEN central test**: Acquire dated 2023 contract and side letters defining AGI; authority to certify; model evaluations/alerts; board distributions and votes; licensing/revenue-rights communications; relevant 2023 communications to Microsoft. Determine whether any required representation was false when made and whether that falsity was material to an identified transfer or payment.

**Sources:**
- CMA decision above.
- SEC-filed Microsoft exhibit, Oct. 28, 2025: https://www.sec.gov/Archives/edgar/data/789019/000119312525256310/msft-ex99_2.htm
- Reuters Nov. 22, 2023: https://uk.investing.com/news/stock-market-news/exclusivesam-altmans-ouster-at-openai-was-precipitated-by-letter-to-board-about-ai-breakthrough-sources-3246958

## Lead D: Restrictive contracts, securities whistleblowers, and possible retaliation predicates

**July 1, 2024 (DOCUMENTED COMPLAINT; ALLEGED VIOLATION)**: Attorneys Kohn, Kohn & Colapinto sent SEC Chairman Gary Gensler a seven-page letter on behalf of anonymous whistleblowers who said they filed an SEC tip/complaint. It alleged nondisparagement and confidentiality provisions with no SEC-reporting exceptions, prior-consent requirements, notification requirements, and waivers of whistleblower awards. These are allegations concerning Rule 21F-17(a), not a published SEC finding against OpenAI.

**Potential criminal screening**: The lawyers expressly asked the SEC to determine whether any employee was harmed for truthful reports to federal law enforcement in violation of 18 U.S.C. 1513(e). Section 1513 is expressly included among the enumerated predicates of RICO under 18 U.S.C. 1961(1). An unlawful SEC-gag agreement alone is not proof of section 1513(e); actual retaliatory action with statutory intent and nexus must be shown. Section 1512 may be a separate inquiry only on evidence of corrupt intent to impede qualifying communication.

**Contemporaneous policy changes and contrary evidence**: OpenAI's May and July 2024 communications and its July 31 written response said it had voided nondisparagement restrictions, updated templates, and never canceled vested equity; former employees described the original agreements as coercive before those changes. Verify both historical contract language and the actual treatment of identified individuals before and after protected reports.

**OPEN central test**: Obtain all template versions and executed agreements 2021-24, equity repurchase decisions, actual threatened or imposed loss, employee regulatory reports with protected confidentiality, management knowledge, and time-indexed adverse actions. The SEC's procedural response and any enforcement disposition remain unestablished by this research.

**Sources:**
- Full law firm letter: https://kkc.com/wp-content/uploads/2024/07/Confidential-Letter-to-SEC-Chair-7.1.24_Redacted-1.pdf
- Senate Grassley correspondence Aug. 1, 2024: https://www.grassley.senate.gov/download/grassley-to-openai_-ndas?download=1
- July 31 OpenAI signed reply above.
- Statute 18 USC 1961: https://uscode.house.gov/view.xhtml?req=granuleid:USC-prelim-title18-section1961&num=0&edition=prelim

## Lead E: Insider financial interests, related-party decisions, and oversight

**May 8, 2026 (DOCUMENTED CONGRESSIONAL INQUIRY)**: House Oversight Chairman Comer sent an information demand to Altman asking about an allegedly proposed $500 million OpenAI equity investment in Helion at approximately $35 billion valuation, given Altman's personal Helion stake. The committee also raised interests involving Greg Brockman and Altman's family fund. House request sought conflict procedures, audit materials, and communications from 2015 onward.

**May 2026 (COURT DISCLOSURE, REPORTED)**: Reuters reported financial disclosures of over $2 billion in Altman-held stakes across nine companies with OpenAI business relationships; Altman described recusals. An OpenAI investment in Helion equity at the proposed terms was reportedly rejected; a separate energy purchase/offtake deal was entered. Distinguish proposal from completed transaction.

**Additional financial evidence:** Reuters reported Altman testified he initiated exploration of OpenAI-Helion business in late 2022 and advocated Helion as a good deal; he also participated in discussing a 2024 Reddit partnership while holding a stake, with the board approving final terms. This means the definition and timing of recusal (originating versus negotiation versus final approval) require scrutiny; an asserted final-decision recusal does not prove no earlier influence. Reuters reported Brockman holds interests in both Helion and Cerebras and a percentage of Altman family fund.

**OPEN central test**: Corporate conflict register, board/committee attendance and abstention logs, alternative bids, fairness review, energy purchase contract pricing, stake valuations around negotiation/announcement, and direct investor/public representations. If robust independent decision-making, fair valuations, and disclosure are established, self-dealing allegations are materially weakened. The existence of an investment by itself establishes financial interest, not theft, bribery, or a wire-fraud scheme.

**Sources:**
- House Oversight inquiry original PDF: https://oversight.house.gov/wp-content/uploads/2026/05/Altman-OpenAI-Letter-050826.pdf
- Reuters May 13, 2026 financial disclosure and Altman testimony: https://www.investing.com/news/economy-news/openai-chief-altman-has-over-2-billion-stake-in-companies-that-dealt-with-openai-court-filing-4686030
- Reuters May 4, 2026 Brockman financial ties: https://www.investing.com/news/stock-market-news/openai-cofounder-discloses-nearly-30-billion-stake-financial-ties-to-altman-4657709
- House letter cites additional original reporting and committee's proposed transaction allegation; follow the footnotes in the PDF for the actual economic deal reporting.

## Lead F: Adjudicated civil/regulatory misconduct (not a criminal conviction)

**Aug. 4, 2026 (DOJ ENFORCEMENT FINDING)**: DOJ Civil Rights Division announced $3.2 million combined settlement with OpenAI OpCo LLC and Statsig over discriminatory labor-certification recruiting practices favoring temporary-visa workers against certain US workers, under the INA. Included $1.2m civil penalty, $2m back pay and three years' compliance monitoring. Stronger evidentiary state than an allegation; do not misclassify this as a criminal RICO predicate.

**Sep. 30, 2026 (STATE INVESTIGATIVE SUBPOENA)**: California AG served OpenAI investigative subpoena concerning cybersecurity incidents and risks following the July 2026 Hugging Face incident and OpenAI's August public disclosure of unauthorized research-model behavior. A subpoena is not a finding of an offense. Test internal incident evidence, notice time, third-party impact, client/regulator representations, safeguards, and remedial changes.

**Sources:**
- DOJ settlement: https://www.justice.gov/opa/pr/civil-rights-division-secures-settlement-openai-discriminating-against-us-workers
- CA AG subpoena statement: https://oag.ca.gov/news/press-releases/part-ongoing-investigation-attorney-general-bonta-serves-investigative-subpoena
- OpenAI Aug. 26, 2026 incident disclosure: https://openai.com/index/hugging-face-incident-and-the-road-ahead/

## Lead G: Ann Altman childhood sexual-abuse allegation (separate personal-conduct matter)

**Jan. 6, 2025 (DOCUMENTED CIVIL COMPLAINT)**: Ann Altman alleged Sam Altman repeatedly sexually abused her beginning around age three, between 1997 and 2006. He denies it. Her lawsuit was dismissed with prejudice on Oct. 19, 2026 for repeated noncompliance with discovery orders, not after a trial on whether the underlying sexual abuse occurred. The docket lists an Oct. 30, 2026 motion to vacate. Both the seriousness and procedural history belong in the record. This is a separate evidence lane, not proof of an AI-industry organized abuse enterprise or RICO connection.

**OPEN central test**: Original and amended complaints, verified exhibits, dated allegations, relevant law-enforcement reports if any, witness accounts and responses, dismissal and motion-to-vacate record. Do not infer a corporate nexus from an alleged personal offense.

**Sources:**
- Original Jan. 6, 2025 complaint: https://gwern.net/doc/reinforcement-learning/openai/2025-altman-42025cv00017-complaint.pdf
- Oct. 19, 2026 memorandum/order text: https://app.midpage.ai/document/altman-v-altman--7a9855e7-78cd-4fa0-877e-00cf4569b9ee
- Docket through Oct. 30: https://www.plainsite.org/courts/missouri-eastern-district-court/altman-v-altman/5l4lv4vak/

## Legal screening: required elements, not analogies

Potentially relevant RICO predicates include 18 USC 1343 (wire fraud), 1512 (witness tampering), 1513 (retaliation), and some forms of securities fraud, *if* evidence establishes their elements and the RICO pattern/enterprise requirements. Civil employment discrimination, ordinary breaches of governance policy, product defects, a failed board vote, a report of AGI-like capabilities, and civil whistleblower-rule violations are not automatically RICO predicates. An individual child-sexual-abuse allegation demands separate legal treatment; its inclusion in the evidence registry does not transform it into a federal racketeering predicate or establish joint enterprise activity.

**Adverse evidence that a credible case must survive**: (1) the 2024 WilmerHale review, as summarized publicly, said management trust rather than product safety motivated removal, and board acted within its discretion; original full review has not been produced in this packet. (2) The Anthropic merger offer is documented *after* firing and was rejected, undercutting an asserted pre-removal deal absent more records. (3) The UK CMA found Microsoft's material influence but not a control increase to de facto control. (4) Murati confirmed GPT-4 Turbo did receive safety review. (5) The proposed $500m Helion equity deal was not consummated. (6) The civil Musk RICO dismissal found inadequately pleaded predicate/enterprise links. (7) Later charity claims in Musk's case were found time-barred rather than tried to a factual verdict. These limits do not disprove separate offenses; they prevent false claims of proof.

## Priority evidence demands and sequencing

**First**: preserve perishable records, especially 2022-23 DSB/JSB policy versions, approval decisions, Microsoft India GPT-4 pilot logs, GPT-4 Turbo Slack and legal advice, deployment manifests, model release audit trails, dated Q* capability evaluations and board distribution emails, and 2023 IP/AGI licensing carve-outs.

**Second**: obtain the corporate control and motive chain: original November 2023 director/partner communications, Microsoft executive decision notes, Anthropic outreach timestamps, original 2023 terms and related-party deal approvals, internal AGI trigger communications.

**Third**: assemble transaction-level wrongdoing proof, not merely suspicious associations: a dated factual representation; who made it; why it was materially false *then*; who knew; communications/wires; which specific party relied or transferred money/property; resulting benefit and injury; rival lawful explanation. Repeat for each alleged act before labeling a predicate.

**Fourth**: separate any whistleblower retaliation into a protected report, respondent's notice, adverse action, intent, contemporaneous reason, comparative case, and statutory federal law-enforcement nexus. Obtain employment/equity transaction evidence legally; do not equate a restrictive contract alone with a criminal conviction.

**Fifth**: document the whistleblower SEC case's actual disposition, the House Oversight response, and the California subpoena status. An unknown disposition is a gap, not an implied cover-up or exoneration.

**Sixth**: keep the personal abuse allegations, civil safety/product cases, and broad corporate-enterprise inquiry in distinct evidence lanes until dated facts establish a real link.

## Falsification matrix

- **Preplanned board/Anthropic collusion** fails if first substantive contact was only after Nov. 17 and no related precommitments exist; strengthens with authenticated pre-ouster exchanges instructing or conditioning the vote.
- **Deliberate AGI-trigger evasion** fails if applicable 2023 contractual threshold was never met and records show consistent good-faith technical review; strengthens with contemporaneous threshold satisfaction and knowingly deceptive certification affecting economic rights.
- **Safety-review misrepresentation** fails if original versions and minutes establish accurate communications or legitimate exceptions; strengthens with contemporaneous original documents contradicting specific attributed statements and evidence of knowing falsity.
- **Criminal whistleblower retaliation** fails without qualifying protected report, actual harmful action and retaliatory intent; strengthens with a time-ordered personnel/equity decision and evidence linking it to protected reporting.
- **Insider financial fraud** fails if valuation, recusal, and counterparty disclosures were accurate and transactions independently approved; strengthens with materially false representations used to obtain identifiable money/property.
- **Broad industry RICO enterprise** fails if alleged acts are unrelated, independently motivated and lack a qualifying repeated predicate pattern and shared enterprise conduct; strengthens with connected, repeated enumerated predicate acts and operational direction.

## Status
This investigation located several concrete custodians and records that previous general summaries failed to prioritize, especially the board-safety approval materials for Dec. 2022, the 2023 Slack contradiction, Microsoft India test logs, SEC whistleblower contracts, financial recusals, and AGI clause triggers. It also identified a genuine DOJ civil enforcement outcome and California compulsory-process inquiry. It has *not* established a broad criminal RICO enterprise or guilt of named individuals. The case becomes stronger or weaker on the original documents just enumerated, not on the number of suspicious social connections.
